**Authority:** this file is the authoritative current-evidence ledger for the milestones in
[the programme roadmap](roadmap.md). The roadmap and the [milestone files](milestones/) define the
planned work and the objective exit gates. This ledger records whether those gates have evidence.

  runtime and compiler, which rule I1 admits for shipping. Both engines'
  changes are merged into their default branches *(2026-10-13; read "Neither engine's change is
  merged into its default branch yet;")*;

**2026-10-11, later the same day: every decision point is taken, and no milestone moves.** The
owner, who holds every role, took the recommendation of each decision that has one, and chose (a)
for SJL-D-13, SJL-D-14 and SJL-D-23, whose recommendations chose no single alternative
([decisions.md](decisions.md)). The decision rows below move from `open` to `taken`. Each names the
work packages that file its record, and **a gate clause that needs a decision is met only by that
record**. Three consequences show in the milestone rows:

- **SJL-D-7's record has a date.** Until SJL-0.4 files its JSD, JSD-0005's stop condition still
  takes the Broiler.JS seed snapshot on 2026-11-30.
- **SJL-3 is no longer a point at which the programme can stop.** SJL-D-23 was taken before the
  pilot: continue. Update rule 10 is revised to match.
- **Collation is milestone SJL-17**, after SJL-9, under SJL-D-24 (c). Its row is added,
  `Not started`.

No code, record or bundle exists, so every milestone stays `Not started`. This paragraph replaces
none; the one above still holds.

paragraphs beside ADR 0011's standing refusals and beside roadmap section 8's table; route [MVP-16](../mvp.md#5-routes-taken-without-a-decision);
the dated corrections of the core ledger and the README; and the JavaScript profile's [JSD-0063](../../src/Broiler.VM.Profile.JavaScript/docs/decisions/0063-shared-libraries-admission-and-the-seed-copy-withdrawn.md)
with correction entries JSC-291 to JSC-295. **Decision `SJL-D-7`'s record is filed before

  when run again from the same binary. All four are an addition of a NaN with a payload, where the bytecode form and the
  value form gave different bits. The bundle keeps both runs and records the finding.

**The owner's words.** A survey of both front ends for SJL-12's plan had been started at the orchestrator's
recommendation: SJL-12 feeds SJL-13 on the critical path, and may start after SJL-0. On 2026-10-19 the owner wrote
"Continue with SJL-12 after the plan is ready", and then "Continue with SJL-12 after the workflows finish". This
ledger reads them together as the choice of SJL-12. They choose the milestone and lift no gate. SJL-9, SJL-11 and
SJL-17 do not move.

**The owner's direction.** On 2026-10-18 the owner wrote: "Please do all recommended decisions and skip waiting for
review." Under it, each decision SJL-12's plan left open, D1 to D42, is taken as the plan recommends and recorded in
[JSD-0080](../../src/Broiler.VM.Profile.JavaScript/docs/decisions/0080-the-shared-frontend-contract-and-the-in-place-preparation.md)
as the owner's decision of 2026-10-19, and the programme proceeds through SJL-12's work packages without pausing for
the owner between them. **It changes no rule of the programme.** `Accepted` still needs review (update rule 7), rules
still come before runs, and no gate moves.

**SJL-12 cannot exit before SJL-3 does.** Its milestone waits on SJL-3 to exit, because the contract cites SJL-3's
literal-value operations, and SJL-3 is still not met: clause 7 waits on a controlled lane this programme does not yet
have, and clause 13 on a later dated choice. Update rule 10 does not say this: it leaves SJL-12's row out only because
SJL-12 may start from SJL-0. So SJL-12 may not meet its gate before SJL-3 meets SJL-3's, whatever its own clauses show.

that carries Broiler.JS's rule, with negative tests, in place of "SJL-0's base": a dated revision of the work
package's done-when, quoting the old text in the milestone, and not a gate revision, since exit clause 2 names no

`49ea00e` on its clauses 1 and 3 (bundle [`sjl-3-001`](../evidence/sjl-3-001/README.md), section 7), and is not run

[`decision-rule-revised-2026-10-20.md`](../evidence/sjl-3-001/decision-rule-revised-2026-10-20.md), written after

  family on `0.1.0-preview.7`, but for paths the command-line root does not build. So the revision judges this family
  at its later sources, with the changes of SJL-4 to SJL-8 and SJL-10 and the shared stream's re-pins since
  `49ea00e`, against SJL-0's base; it does not judge SJL-3's cut-over alone, and a NOT MET cannot be laid on that
  cut-over alone either. Arm B, the workloads, the repetitions, the allowances, the A/A floor as the only tolerance,
  and the MET line are the original's.
- **`to-string.js`'s condition** is its own arm's answer, held to the specification by a transcript check against the
  shared corpus's own reference, Broiler.JS's `scripts/conformance/numref.py`, because the cut-over's radix
  corrections move its answer. The other workloads and every control keep the base arm's answer, and each was
  checked at those sources before the revision was written ([`revision-check/`](../evidence/sjl-3-001/revision-check/README.md)).
- **It is the second and last measurement of part C.** A NOT MET is not followed by a third under a further
  revision, and the tolerance is not changed.

  line hold, but on some workloads and forms the after arm is not within the A/A floor of the base
  ([`measurement-revised/`](../evidence/sjl-3-001/measurement-revised/README.md), and the bundle's section 10). Clause
  13 stays open after the second and last measurement its revision allows.
- **What a reading says.** The A/A floor is part C's only tolerance, and under it a change that makes no difference
  reads NOT MET more often than MET. So a NOT MET on clause 3 alone is no finding of a slowdown, and a MET is no speed
  claim, in either direction. Nothing here is compared with Broiler.JS.

  decision D14 is carried for the profile owner; this ledger does not judge JSB-1 (update rule 6).

option reads in specification order, its errors and its locale policies. **Plural ranges and display-name naming are
adoptions** (X1): Broiler.JS implements neither. **Decisions SJL-D-22 and SJL-D-24 are recorded**: under SJL-D-22 (a)
the generator writes a list per embedding, this family's not growing, so its table stays byte for byte under rule N28,

deletes `JsParser` and the binder's copies of the library's checks, and the shared tree itself at step 2. ADR 0011's
refusal of "a shared syntax tree or parser" is read, by ADR 0014's scope reading, as not reaching the library.

**2026-11-01, later again: SJL-17.1 is filed in this repository.** The area verdict is
[JSD-0084](../../src/Broiler.VM.Profile.JavaScript/docs/decisions/0084-collation-moves-to-the-shared-intl-library.md),
decided on 2026-11-01: **collation is accepted**, the Unicode Collation Algorithm over CLDR's root collation and each
embedding's tailorings, with `sensitivity`, `ignorePunctuation`, `numeric` and `caseFirst`, moving to
`Broiler.ECMAScript.Intl` as `UcaCollator`, over the collation tables the data home's generator writes. **It is an
adoption** (X1): Broiler.JS implements no collation algorithm and hands its strings to the host, so the shared
collator is this family's, which Broiler.JS adopts for the locales and collations its covered list names. Each engine
keeps its `Intl.Collator` objects, its option reads in specification order, `localeCompare`'s coercion, its errors and
its locale providers. This family's tables stay byte for byte under rule N28, and its locale list does not change.

**The owner's direction.** On 2026-10-18 the owner wrote: "Please do all recommended decisions and skip waiting for
review." Under it, on 2026-11-01, each decision SJL-17's plan left open was taken as the plan recommends, E1 to E14, and
JSD-0084 records each as the owner's decision. None was decided again; where the correspondence found a drifted
citation or a reading the plan's text leaves open, the decision says so beside it. **It changes no rule of the
programme.** `Accepted` still needs review (update rule 7), rules still come before runs, and no gate moves. Among the
decisions:
- E2: X1 is an adoption;
- E3: Broiler.JS's shared collator covers root and the tailorings for `de` (phonebook, search), root `search`, `en`,
  `es`, `sv` and `th`, per locale and collation; CJK and the rest stay on its host provider;
- E4: under invariant globalization no locale collates by code unit;
- E6 and E7: normalization through Broiler.Unicode, and the caller's meter at this family's points and in its units, so
  there is no class C2;
- E8: Broiler.JS's bound `compare` is cached;
- E10: no part in a zone with daylight-saving time, and no measurement;
- E11: both engines' deletion lists;
- E14: one merge per engine, the bases after SJL-9's after commits.

- **The binder's pattern names.** In `JsBinder.Walks.cs`, a `BindingIdentifier` reached under `Use.Value` is counted as
  a reference, the pattern's name pushed as the adapted tree pushed its leaf's name expression. The fix proposed is a
  dedicated `Use` routed through `VisitLeaf`. It is a product change after step 2: if it moves the binder's facts it
  needs its own artifact and test262 check, it changes neither step's merged window, `JsBinder` is deleted at SJL-15.3,
  and it lands outside SJL-11.4's window. No record held it before this entry.

  than the workflow asks for (that bundle's section 7). Decision D37 freezes both repositories' evidence workflows for
  SJL-13 to SJL-15, and a root `global.json` would change the SDK of every later base and after build, SJL-11.4's
  admission commit's among them; it is for the owner, with its alternative.

  are `x86-64-sysv`, so the ratchet refuses them and compares no row; the release gate's row G9 needs a native run on
  `win-x64` held to the floor, which the evidence workflow cannot give without a change decision D37 bars, or the

- **This family's defect, row S8a (decision D-wiv).** Under `iv`, this family's matcher builds `\w` unfolded as an
  operand of a `v`-mode class, so a class difference with a folded operand keeps `\w`'s members that case-fold to
  another code point; the text folds `\w` first. The correspondence's row S8a records it as a correction of this
  family, kind R, which SJL-11.4 makes, and row S8's cell is narrowed by a dated note. No test262 file at the pin and no
  vector of the pinned corpus reaches it.
- **The rule's revision of 2026-11-16.** Bundle [`sjl-11-001`](../evidence/sjl-11-001/README.md)'s part A names its
  kind R corrections by a fixed list, so
  [`decision-rule-area-revised-2026-11-16.md`](../evidence/sjl-11-001/decision-rule-area-revised-2026-11-16.md), filed
  before any run of the rule, adds row S8a to it and changes nothing else. It does not admit the owner's workflow change
  of 2026-10-30 and does not drop row S5; both stay the owner's, by a further dated file before any run.
- **Broiler.JS's differences no row named (decision D-jsrows).** They are rows S12 to S15, M8 and M9 of the same
  correspondence, corrections of Broiler.JS, grouped by Broiler.JS's answer and by the arbiter's reason, with the two
  inputs the reduction reached in row S14; row M4 is widened to its own vectors that the arms reached, and some vectors
  the arms' classifier left in class (c) rows S7 and S8 already name. They are filed here because Broiler.JS's rule
  classifies by this correspondence. No test262 file Broiler.JS's bundle `sjl-10-001` fails is explained by them, so no
  Broiler.JS part A row is known to move; a Broiler.JS rule revision, should one be needed, is Broiler.JS's to file.
- **The inputs the arbiter cannot reach.** JSD-0081 and the bundle's README record what Broiler.RegEx's reduction of
  2026-11-15 found and which class each set stays in under the reference-arm rule as written. That rule is
  Broiler.RegEx's, so whether the inputs whose difference does not survive the reduction may leave class (c), and
  whether the library's stop at its own ceiling may be classed other than (c), are its owner's to decide; nothing here
  decides them.
- **JSD-0081's falsifier.** Its note takes the reading that a difference the arbiter decides is classified when a row
  names its input and classes the engine the arbiter shows wrong, as the new rows do, and that an input the arbiter
  cannot reach does not falsify it. The reading not taken is that "classifies" means the arms' classification listing
  the difference outside class (c).

**SJL-11: the third classification round.** The classifier `@7` read rows M10 to M15 and left in class (c) the `exec`
answers of Broiler.JS that no row names, with P3's `O06`. They were read again by mechanism in arm J's copy of
Broiler.JS at `1f91b8f1` and, where the pattern reaches Broiler.Regex `0.1.0-preview.4` or its parser decides the route,
in preview.4's source, and each mechanism was checked outside this repository by running arm J's copy, or .NET on the
translated pattern, with the construct written another way. Under the owner's direction of 2026-10-18, JSD-0081's
decision D-jsrest of 2026-11-16 is taken as recommended, with the alternatives not taken:
- **Rows M16 to M26**, corrections of Broiler.JS, one row to a mechanism, rows M16, M17, M18 and M19 corrections of
  preview.4 too: `\c` with no control letter, which preview.4 reads as `\`; a group name with `\u{…}`, which preview.4
  refuses, so that .NET's loop answers; `\k<…>` with `\u{…}`, which preview.4 refuses too and the translator writes as
  no back-reference; a legacy octal escape preview.4 misreads, so that `GapScan` does not route a nullable quantifier;
  the same escape before `\d` in a class; `\S` in a class under `RegexOptions.ECMAScript`; an empty group under a lazy
  quantifier; `^` or `$` in a class under `m`; a back-reference to a group with no capture without
  `RegexOptions.ECMAScript`; a letter expanded inside a modifier group that removes `i`; and a class with `\W`, `\p` or
  `\P` under `i`. Each preview.4 row notes that Broiler.JS routes to `0.1.0-preview.6` since
  Broiler-Platform/Broiler.JS#1119, whose parser reads those constructs as the text does, by reading.
- **Dated notes** on rows M8 (`\b` and `\B` outside a class lexed as the text lexes it), M12 (a `-` before `\p` or
  `\P`), M13 (its reading where the nested class is not the class's last element, which was undefined: .NET refuses
  such a pattern, so the row takes no input of it; and its nested class lexed as .NET lexes it) and M15 (the invariant
  answer up to row M10's names).
- **What it corrects:** JSD-0081's note under D-jsexec that "by reading most of them are answered by Broiler.Regex's
  preview.4, to which `GapScan` routes them" did not hold: run with a report of its route, arm J's copy hands few of
  them to preview.4, and most stay on .NET, several because preview.4's parser refuses or misreads a construct the text
  accepts. JSD-0081 notes it beside the words.
- **What it leaves:** the inputs of pattern `P4-47050`, whose mechanism is not read in full, stay in class (c), named by
  no row, so on the reading JSD-0081 takes its falsifier's first clause stands for them, and the owner's choice is open.
  `O06` stays row B1's, and how a row-named input the arbiter cannot reach is classed is the reference-arm rule owner's,
  in Broiler.RegEx; this repository files nothing there. The arms' class (c) stays non-empty until Broiler.RegEx's
  classifier reads the new rows and notes. No rule file of bundle `sjl-11-001` changes: these are Broiler.JS's rows and
  move no part A or B verdict of this family's.

**SJL-11: row M27.** That input's mechanism is read, in arm J's copy of Broiler.JS at `1f91b8f1` and in .NET's source
at dotnet/runtime `v10.0.15`, the runtime the copy was run on:
- Without `RegexOptions.ECMAScript`, under `s` or with a modifier group, .NET parses `\b` as its `Boundary`.
- The translator's `\D` is stored by .NET as the negated range `[^0-9]`.
- .NET's test of whether a loop's set lies inside `\w` enumerates the set's ranges without reading its negation. So .NET
  makes the loop before `\b` atomic, and a lazy one greedy, where the text tries `\b` after each iteration.

It was checked outside this repository by running arm J's copy on the pattern with `\b` written `(?<=\b)`, and with
`\D+?` written `(?:(?!\d)[^])+?`, both of which the text reads the same; each gives the text's answer in every culture.
- **Row M27** names the mechanism, a correction of Broiler.JS, under JSD-0081's decision D-jsrest, whose own rule, one
  row to a mechanism, gives it. No new decision is taken.
- **How it takes an input:** like rows M16, M21, M23 and M25, only where the arbiter's answer to its reading is
  Broiler.JS's. It is read after M26, so row M8 keeps the inputs of the same construct its list takes.
- **Records:** the correspondence's introduction, class list and bundle README carry dated paragraphs. JSD-0081 carries
  dated notes under D-jsrest, in its summary, sub-area M and X6. Its "Falsified if" note now reads that once
  Broiler.RegEx's classifier reads row M27, no difference the arms found remains named by no row, and until then the
  clause stands for these inputs.
- **What it leaves:** no rule file of bundle `sjl-11-001` changes. Row M27 is Broiler.JS's and moves no part A or B
  verdict of this family's. The arms' class (c) stays non-empty until Broiler.RegEx's classifier reads the row.

**The owner's own choice.** Part C's revision of 2026-11-18 lets a lane collector run `collect.py`'s `measure-revised`
step and depart from it "only in these", four departures, and says "It departs in nothing else". That step starts the
program by the name the base collector gives it, `Broiler.VM.Composition.JavaScript.Cli`, with no extension, which is
the apphost's name on `linux-x64` and not on `win-x64`. On 2026-11-18 the owner was asked: "Broiler.VM's collector
(collect.py) starts the program as "…\Broiler.VM.Composition.JavaScript.Cli", without ".exe". On Windows the launch will
probably fail, because ".Cli" reads as a file extension and Windows does not add ".exe" to a full path. Adding ".exe" is
not one of the four collector departures the 2026-11-18 revision allows. The brief has the local session test this
first, without running either version, and stop if it fails. Should the rule allow it before your run, so the
measurement isn't refused partway through?" The owner chose "Allow .exe now": "Before any run, I file a dated note on
the 2026-11-18 revision. It reads the program's file name on win-x64 as carrying ".exe", the RID's own naming, the same
way the revision already reads the native backend. The four departures and everything else stay as they are, and the
note quotes what it reads." The option not taken was "Probe first, then decide": "Nothing changes now. The local session
tests the launch, and if it fails, it stops and the question comes back to you then." It is the owner's own dated choice
of 2026-11-18, not one taken under the owner's direction of 2026-10-18, and the note quotes the question and both
options whole.

- **3** (2026-11-18). Asked: "SJL-11.5's base in Broiler.JS needs two things only you can order. First, its inputs
  commits (24fe7cba, 189d65ff) and a new dated rule revision must land on main by a merge commit before the base is
  taken. Second, test262-evidence.yml moved from ubuntu-latest to ubuntu-24.04 in your dependency update of 2026-10-29

  before any run can admit that change. Do you allow both?" Chosen: "Allow both" — "The inputs land on main by a merge
  commit with one dated revision. The revision lists the y-flag correction row and admits the ubuntu-24.04 workflow.
  Then the hold starts and the base runs are taken at main's head." Not taken: "Inputs only" — "The inputs land, but
  the workflow change is not admitted. Part A's base can then only be taken with the workflow as the rule's commit
  carries it."; "Not yet" — "Nothing lands on main for SJL-11.5. Its base and merge wait."
- **4** (2026-11-19). Asked: "SJL-11.5's part B (the RegExpBenchmarks measurement) runs arm B (base commit) and arm A

  happen to part B?" Chosen: "On your lane after merge" — "After the merge, a local session on win-x64-r7-5800x builds
  arm B from 5429c406 and arm A from the merge and runs part B as written, as SJL-3's part B was run. A dated note reads
  arm B as built from the base commit, not as a run taken before the merge." Not taken: "Record it void" — "No part B
  run. It is recorded void, which by the rule leaves the measurement question open for this engine. It does not block
  anything."
- **5** (2026-11-19). Asked: "Part D's base runs read the corpus at revision 43, but the rule names revision 32. The
  regexp vectors' ids, inputs and expected outputs are the same in both; only […] class lines and the divergence lines
  changed, at revision 39. How should part D be read, at both the base and the after runs?" Chosen: "Read revision 32" —

  new runs needed, and the after runs are read the same way." Not taken: "Read revision 43" — "Read the tree's revision
  43 at both, with the equivalence of ids, inputs and outputs recorded. This departs from the rule's named revision."
- **6** (2026-11-19). Asked: "Decision D35 orders both cut-overs "after SJL-12's after runs and after the candidate
  reading". The candidate reading is taken and merged (Broiler.Regex #28, #29, #30), but Regex task 4.2 stays open until
  the scratch branch sjl-11-2-control-b is deleted. Is D35 met for SJL-11.5's merge?" Chosen: "Yes, the reading is
  taken" — "D35 asks only that the candidate reading come first, and it has. SJL-11.5 may merge once its PR's CI is
  green and the hold still holds." Not taken: "No, close task 4.2 first" — "SJL-11.5 waits until you delete the scratch
  branch and task 4.2 is recorded closed. The hold stays on meanwhile."
- **7** (2026-11-19). Asked: "SJL-11.5 part B's arm A is "the after commit, with the same two files" (RegExpBenchmarks.cs
  and the benchmark Program.cs "as the inputs commit has them"). RegExpBenchmarks.cs is unchanged at the merge. But the
  inputs commit's Program.cs calls the RegexTiering metrics that the cut-over deletes (D20/D21), so it cannot build at
  the merge. How should arm A be built?" Chosen: "Merge's own Program.cs" — "A dated revision, filed before any part B
  run, reads arm A's Program.cs as the merge's own. That file differs from the inputs commit's only by the removed
  --regex-tiering block, which part B never invokes. Its --regexp-answers path and the benchmark entry must be
  byte-identical; if they aren't, the run stops and comes back to you." Not taken: "Inputs file minus the block" — "Arm
  A uses the inputs commit's Program.cs with only the --regex-tiering block cut out, as a patched file named by hash in
  the manifest. It stays closer to the rule's words, but arm A then builds from a file no commit has."; "Record part B
  void" — "No part B run. The rule then leaves the measurement question open for this engine. It blocks nothing."

  base, the old .NET-translated engine ran N05 past the […] wall-clock limit and it was killed. After the cut-over, N05

**SJL-15: decision D-ab1.** The entry of 2026-11-17 ends its list of what the `semantics` vectors' first runs show of
this family with "where row AB1 stands in the rule is not decided here". Row AB1, Annex B.3.3's block-function aliases,
is a correction of Broiler.VM across both engines that SJL-15.3 answers, and test262's
`annexB/language/function-code/block-decl-func-skip-arguments.js` at `ccaac100` asserts this family's answer, an
earlier edition's reading; so at window 1 that file's verdict moves from pass to fail, which bundle `sjl-15-001`'s part
A reads as a regression, and an accepted input's bytes move outside part B's class P. Decision D-ab1, taken on
2026-11-23 as recommended under the owner's direction of 2026-10-18 and recorded in
[JSD-0088](../../src/Broiler.VM.Profile.JavaScript/docs/decisions/0088-static-semantics-move-to-the-shared-frontend.md)'s
section "The owner's decisions of 2026-11-23", takes alternative (a): bundle `sjl-15-001`'s rule is revised a third
time, before any run, by
[`decision-rule-area-revised-2026-11-23.md`](../evidence/sjl-15-001/decision-rule-area-revised-2026-11-23.md), which
quotes the rule whole and leaves it and both revisions of 2026-11-16 unedited. At window 1 only:
- part A gains class ED: the variants of that file whose verdict moves from pass to fail because SJL-15.3 follows the
  pinned edition, and any other variant whose verdict moves for row AB1's reason, a move from pass to fail only where
  the variant asserts the base's answer against the pinned edition's text, each listed with the row. Class ED is not
  class C1 widened: C1's correction half (LB1, MR2 and the dynamic function's goals) admits only a move from fail to
  pass, and its (T) half is row PN1's timing and is unchanged. For the listed variants class ED departs from template
  T1 of the [validation document](validation.md) twice, both named before any run: T1 admits no move from pass to fail
  outside C2, and class ED is none of that document's classes C1 to C5, of which T1's MET line reads "every changed
  verdict falls in C1 to C5 and is listed". Class C3, "Implementation-defined", is not used: the rule reads "no C2, no
  C3 and no C5", and with Annex B on in both engines' requests the alias is the pinned edition's text;
- part B's class F gains row AB1, beside LB1, MR2 and the dynamic function's goals: an accepted input whose artifact
  differs only for row AB1's reason, listed with its decoded difference as class P's are.

Every other line of the rule and of its revisions stands, parts G and J among them: part G's twins and part J's
workloads name part B's class P alone, so a twin or a workload whose artifact moves for row AB1 is read under those

## 1. Reading this ledger

The three categories Broiler.VM's core ledger keeps apart are kept apart here:

- **Plan** is proposed scope, sequencing, ownership or an exit gate. It is not evidence.
- **Observed repository state** is a reviewable fact about a checkout. It can explain a status but
  cannot by itself satisfy a gate.
- **Accepted evidence** is an immutable evidence bundle that satisfies an exit gate. It names the
  exact sources and the gate, records the commands and the environment, keeps their outputs, and
  shows every clause met. Under [`docs/mvp.md`](../mvp.md), no milestone reaches `Accepted` while
  review is deferred.

**This ledger spans repositories, and it keeps them apart by naming which is which.** Every exit
gate in this programme is split into three groups of clauses:

- Broiler.VM clauses;
- Broiler.JS clauses;
- component clauses (Broiler.Unicode and Broiler.RegEx). *(Corrected 2026-10-11: the list read
  "and Broiler.DateTime if chosen". Decision SJL-D-1 placed time data in the Broiler.JS
  repository.)*

Each group is met only by a bundle collected in its own repository. A row here may *link* to
another repository's bundle or status file as observed state of that repository. That link explains
a status and never meets a clause in another repository's group. Broiler.JS keeps its own status
file for its clauses, `docs/roadmap/SharedLibraries.status.md`, created at SJL-0. Neither ledger
advances the other's rows, and neither advances the JavaScript profile's ledger, which records
observed state under its own legend.

**No figure of any run appears here.** A row cites the bundle.

### Status vocabulary

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `Not started` | No milestone-owned code, record or bundle exists in any repository. Planning text does not change this state. |
| `In progress` | The milestone owns code, records or a retained bundle in at least one repository, and at least one clause is unmet. The row names the unmet clauses individually, with their repository. A milestone whose every clause is met on retained evidence, but which is not accepted because review is deferred, also stays `In progress` and says both halves. |
| `Blocked` | A named clause cannot be met because of a decision or precondition another party holds. The row names the holder and the unblock condition, and is `In progress` on the rest. |
| `Refused` | The milestone's area failed the programme's extraction gate (roadmap section 5), and its record is filed. **A met gate for the refusal, and an end for the area.** |
| `Accepted` | Every clause in every repository is covered by accepted evidence, and the owners and a reviewer have recorded a decision. Unreachable while review is deferred. |

`Refused` is the one state Broiler.VM's other ledgers do not use. It is added because this
programme's gate can legitimately end an area. The universal bytecode programme instead records a
refusal through its first milestone's verdict.

that formalises it is not yet filed; the work packages named file it.

All holders are one person, recorded in Broiler.VM as EX-30.

### Observed repository state on this ledger's date

These facts explain why rows are `Not started` and what the first milestones meet. None of them is
evidence for any clause.

Bundle `sjl-0-001` re-derived the inventory at Broiler.VM `0087e8d7`, the base its baselines were
taken at, and at the other repositories' revisions below, which had not moved.

| Repository | Revision | Fact |
|---|---|---|
| Broiler.VM | `88d8b51d` | Rules A1, A2, A3 and N1 refuse every shared package. The roadmap's sections 5, 9 and 12 and the JavaScript profile's invariant 11 refuse any edge to Broiler.JS. |
| Broiler.JS | `44a214dc` | Its documents state that no Broiler.VM integration is planned. Its `FrontEnd/Semantics` boundary is a hypothesis pending its own MOD-M2. |
| Broiler.Unicode | `2affa78f` | Published at preview.3. No roadmap, no identifier series, no assurance annotations. |
| Broiler.RegEx | `c1422f4d` | Published at preview.4. Its budget is reset per start position, and has no caller charge and no cancellation. |
| Broiler.DateTime | `8de12cc3` | No consumer anywhere. Its civil-day primitives are internal in every committed state; a change that makes them public exists only in an uncommitted stash. |

---

## 3. Required evidence bundles

Each repository with clauses retains its bundles under its own `docs/evidence/sjl-n-nnn/`, in
Broiler.VM's bundle shape:

- a `README.md` stating what the bundle demonstrates, what it excludes and what it does not claim;
- a `manifest.json` naming every input, script, package and binary by hash, and the commit each was
  built from. **For every admitted shared package: its identifier, its version, its content hash and
  the source commit from its nuspec** (rule `I7`);
- the retained logs the milestone file lists;
- where the milestone judges a comparison, the `decision-rule.md` committed before the code it
  judges.

A bundle holds figures where it must. No document outside a bundle quotes them. Text files are
stored with LF line endings before they are hashed.

---

## 4. Update rules

1. **Same change.** Update this ledger in the same change that lands, blocks, refuses, supersedes or
   narrows a milestone's claim. Keep earlier evidence links and decisions as dated history. Correct
   a sentence by quoting the superseded reading beside the new one, never by editing it away.
2. **No gate copied into the evidence column.** Link the immutable bundle, and state what it
   demonstrated, including failures and exclusions.
3. **No transitive completion.**
   - Broiler.JS's clauses met do not meet Broiler.VM's.
   - A vector agreement does not meet a test262 clause.
   - JIT, trimmed or one-mode success does not meet a Native AOT clause.
   - One area met meets no other area.
4. **`In progress` once anything exists.** A milestone that owns any code, record or bundle, in any
   repository, is `In progress`. A milestone with a clause gated on an open decision is `Blocked`
   on that clause and `In progress` on the rest, and the row says so in those words.
5. **Gate changes.** If a gate changes, record the revision in the milestone file with the
   superseded text quoted, and re-evaluate existing evidence. Evidence gathered for an older
   population is not carried forward silently.
6. **Separate ledgers.** Do not record another ledger's status here, and do not advance another
   ledger's row from here: Broiler.VM's core ledger, the JavaScript profile's ledger, Broiler.JS's
   ledgers, the component repositories' records. A link to another ledger's row is observed state.
7. **`Accepted` needs review.** A milestone moves to `Accepted` only after its owners and a reviewer
   confirm every clause in every repository is covered, with the decision date and the bundle
   identifiers in the row. Under `docs/mvp.md` no row reaches it while review is deferred.
   **Human review gates a release, not a development step.**
8. **No figures.** No count, timing, ratio or score enters this file, whether or not a bundle holds
   it.
9. **Refusals.** A refused area moves to `Refused` in the change that files its verdict. The row
   names the failed condition, what would reopen it, and where the source-level pointers are.
   Reopening is a new dated record and moves the row back to `In progress`.
10. **The pilot decision.** Decision `SJL-D-23` was taken before the pilot: the programme
    continues. Its record is filed at SJL-0, so SJL-3's row moves on SJL-3's own clauses only. The
    rows of SJL-4 to SJL-11, SJL-13 to SJL-15 and SJL-17 meet their gates on the milestones they
    wait on, and on nothing else. *(Revised 2026-10-11, when `SJL-D-23` was taken. The rule read:
    "Decision `SJL-D-23` is recorded in the change that meets or refuses SJL-3. Until it is
    recorded, the rows of SJL-4 to SJL-11 and SJL-13 to SJL-15 may start but may not meet a
    gate.")*
